THIS WRIT APPEAL HAVING BEEN FINALLY HEARD ON 23.01.2019, ALONG
WITH WA.647/2017, AND CONNECTED CASES, THE COURT ON THE SAME
DAY PASSED THE FOLLOWING:

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 182
JUDGMENT
Devan Ramachandran,J
The essential and constitutive issues in
these batch of cases are whether shops and
businesses can be permitted in areas, except
in those which are specified for the said
purpose, under the Detailed Lay Out Plan for
the residential colony by name “Panampilly
Nagar” at Cochin, which was published as per
Circular No. 51227/E3/99/LSGD dated
05.12.1999.
2. The writ petitions, which have led to
these appeals, were heard by a learned
Single Judge of this Court and by judgment
dated 06.01.2017, after an exhaustive

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 183
evaluation of all the relevant and germane
inputs and factors, it was declared that
there cannot be any “user contrary to that
envisaged under the Scheme, more so when the
land acquisition was in its very
implementation” (sic). Consequent directions
were also issued in the said judgment that
establishments which require D&O licence
from the Cochin Corporation, except in
certain plots, shall not be allowed to
function in offence to the provisions of the
said Scheme; with exemption granted to
shops, offices and business establishments
that were functioning in areas specified for
the same as per the said Scheme.
3. The various petitioners, whose
businesses and activities allegedly suffered
on account of the judgment, have filed these
appeals, primarily contending that they were

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 184
all functioning in their respective premises
for a long period of time and that the
Scheme in question suffers from obsolescence
and thus incapable of implementation on
account of efflux of time.
4. Even though, various ancillary
contentions and averments have been made in
these writ appeals, both on law and on
facts, we are now aware that the Government
of Kerala has, pending these appeals,
brought out a further notification, bearing
number G.O No.83/2018/LSGD dated 31.10.2018,
modifying the existing terms of the Detailed
Town Planning Scheme for the Elamkulam West
area of Ernakulam District, which was
originally settled under Section 12(3) of
the Town Planning Act,1932 and notified
through a Government Order dated 15.10.1969
and published as SRO No.449/1969 in the

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 185
Kerala Gazette No.41 dated 21.10.1969.
5. Shorn of unnecessary details, the
afore mentioned new notification converts
the areas in question into a “Mixed User
Zone”, which means that both residential and
commercial activities can be pursued in the
areas, subject, of course, to the requisites
of obtaining licences from the concerned
Local Self Government Institution and such
other concerned statutory Authorities.
6. We are, however, also aware that this
notification has now been challenged in
W.P.(C) No.40090 of 2018; wherein, the
petitioners therein assail its terms as
having been issued contrary to the
provisions of the Town Planning Act and on
various other grounds, including that the
minimum statutory period for notification
has not been complied with by the Government

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 186
before issuing the same.
7. We have narrated the afore essential
facts because, we are guided to the
impression that once a new notification,
mentioned above, has now come into force;
albeit subject to the opinion of this Court
on its legality, which will have to be
decided in the pending writ petition, the
effect of the earlier notification with
respect to the areas in question, confining
it as a “Residential Zone” with certain
specified commercial areas, may not be any
more relevant. This is because under the new
notification, any person who intends to set
up a commercial establishment within the
Panampilly Nagar area or the West Elamkulam
area can approach the competent Authorities
and obtain licences, based on his or her
entitlement and consequently the rigour of

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 187
the earlier Scheme over the areas in
question would now loose its grip.
8. In this perspective, we are of the
view that unless the petitioners therein are
able to obtain reliefs in W.P.(C)
No.40090/2018, wherein the new notification
has been challenged, no purpose would be
served in our considering the merits of the
contentions in these writ appeals,
particularly when the judgment impugned
herein was based on the earlier notification
of the year 1969. Pertinently, the learned
Single Judge, who delivered the impugned
judgment, has relied exclusively on a Scheme
which concededly stipulated the concerned
areas as a “Residential Zone” and therefore,
the views and conclusions therein were also
guided and modulated by the terms of that
Scheme. Since that scheme is no longer in

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 188
existence now, it having been modified by
the new notification afore mentioned, we are
of the view that a further consideration of
the merits of the judgment impugned in these
appeals would not be now necessary or
prudent because the said judgment would have
no bearing prospective to the issuance of
the new notification.
9. We are, therefore, axiomatically
guided to the view that these writ appeals
can now be closed; however, with a rider
that all persons or entities conducting
commercial activities in the areas in
question, will be obligated to approach the
concerned Local Self Government Institution
and such other competent statutory
Authorities and to obtain all necessary and
requisite consents and licenses; without
which, the competent Authorities of the

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 189
Local Self Government Institution will be
obligated to shut them down for violation of
the provisions of the applicable Statues,
Rules and Regulations.
10. Once our mind as afore is indited,
the only remaining question is, whether, in
the event the new notification being set
aside for any reason through the litigative
process, the appellants herein would be able
to approach this Court appropriately,
reviving their right to challenge the
judgment which is now impugned in these
appeals. Even though this is a speculative
scenario, we are of the view that, if for
any reason the new notification is set
aside; resultantly causing the old Scheme to
revive, then obviously, the appellants in
these cases will obtain the locus to
approach this Court appropriately, seeking

WA Nos.647,593,630,652,653,654,655,657,707,
708,709,784,1499/2017 /2017 & 2049/2018 190
ventilation of their grievances, if any, at
that point of time, in terms of law.

JUDGE
